## Scope facts (owner decisions, not legal findings)

- **Employers:** private sector. **Workers:** common-law employees; independent contractors / Form 1099-NEC **OUT OF SCOPE** (owner instruction, 2026-10-29); TODO (2026-11-02): cite register row US-OWN-008 here once agent 2 merges it into `legal-basis-register.csv` (not present at the end of the 2026-11-02 id-rename run). Special populations: US-MQ-12 answered (owner, US-OWN-005: all populations IN).
- **Filing channel:** statutory filings (federal and state) go through a **third-party filing provider; no ADP integration** (owner, biweekly 2026-10-21). The SIR covers the provider handoff, not direct authority submission. Authorisation instruments: US-MQ-09.
- **Structure:** a common FEDERAL layer + a MULTISTATE routing domain + 51 jurisdictions (50 states + DC), all 51 in scope (owner, 2026-10-29; per-jurisdiction questions NOT reduced). Jurisdiction ordering: US-MQ-13 answered (waves 1/2/3, market proxy). Package shape: US-MQ-14 answered (single US package).

## Tier verdict (repeat verbatim in CCG m01, scope_manifest.json and the QA report)

> Overall T4 (discriminator confirmed 2026-10-29 on source-read evidence; per-jurisdiction census completed at seal attempt 3 and re-checked at seal attempts 4, 5 and 6, 2026-10-30 to 2026-11-02; mandate sealed 2026-11-02 with all 65 questions answered; the limbs reopened and closed at seal attempt 6 (US-MQ-26 CT, US-MQ-45 MS, US-MQ-57 OR) bear on no tier; Stage-5 re-check 2026-11-02: superseded citations re-pointed to their S5 successors, census unchanged (new local layers found only inside T4 states: Lexington KY, Greenwood Village and Glendale CO OPT), and the five prevailing-wage limbs reopened that day (US-MQ-30 GA, 32 IA, 45 MS, 47 NC, 64 UT) and closed at the reseal the same day bear on no tier). Employer-operation test: T4 requires a second independent calculation AND a separate remittance/return to a second authority. Federal: 26 U.S.C. 3402(a)(1) obliges every employer to withhold federal income tax under IRS tables and covers federal tax only [US-FED-012]; FICA and FUTA go on federal returns 941/944/940 [US-FED-005, US-FED-007, US-FED-015]. State: a wage-taxing state imposes its own withholding computed on its own basis (e.g. Va. Code 58.1-461 [US-VAPIT-002]) remitted to the state revenue authority on its own returns (Virginia Tax VA-5/VA-6 [US-VAPIT-003]); state UI is a separate state tax base and agency (Fla. Stat. 443.1216 [US-MULTI-001]; SC Code ch. 41-31 [US-SCS5-015]). Nothing is absorbed into the federal deduction (contrast Switzerland, StHG Art. 36a Abs. 1, where one deduction discharges all levels). FUTA is federal but its credit depends on state UI paid and on the state's Title XII loan status (26 U.S.C. 3302(c)(2) [US-FED-014]): a cross-layer dependency, so futa is T3. Inside a state, a local layer is T4 where a locality receives a separate return (City of Portland Metro SHS / Multnomah PFA [US-ORS5-004]; Ohio municipal income tax, ORC 718.03 [US-OHP52-003]; Pennsylvania Act 32 EIT/LST to the local collector [US-PA-004]; Jersey City and Newark employer payroll taxes on quarterly city returns [US-NJP6-001, US-NJP6-002]) and T3 where it is piggybacked on the state return (Maryland county tax on MW506 [US-MDP51-001]; NYC and Yonkers on NYS-45/NYS-1 [US-NY-008]). Consequence: the federal core and each jurisdiction are declared and scoped as parallel layers, and the multistate domain routes each employee to one or more of them. Jurisdiction annexes (re-checked 2026-10-30, seal attempt 5): 9 T1 (no state wage tax; SUI and levies only: AK, FL, NH, NV, SD, TN, TX, WA, WY; WA declares the Seattle payroll expense tax, a threshold-conditional employer tax on a city return [US-WAP51-001], as a T4-shaped element), 31 T3 (state withholding, local layer absent or piggybacked on the state return), 11 T4 (local layer remitted to the locality on its own return: AL occupational taxes [US-ALP51-001], CO Denver OPT [US-COP52-001], DE Wilmington [US-DEP52-001], KY occupational taxes [US-KYP52-002], MI 23 self-administering cities [US-MIP52-002], MO St. Louis/Kansas City [US-MOP52-006], NJ Jersey City/Newark employer payroll taxes filed with the city [US-NJP6-001, US-NJP6-002], OH [US-OHP52-003], OR [US-ORS5-004], PA [US-PA-004], WV municipal service fees withheld and remitted to the city, W. Va. Code 8-13-13 [US-WVR3-001], Charleston [US-WVR3-002]); 0 unclassified; no classification rests on MANUAL/gated evidence.

**Sub-national key:** work_location_jurisdiction (state + locality down to school district / PSD); residence_jurisdiction (state + locality down to school district); sui_state (result of the localization test, US-MQ-10). Checked below the state tier: several local wage taxes are residence-based or school-district-based, so a state-only key cannot express them. Work and residence are separate keys because the multistate rules diverge on them. Confirmed 2026-10-29: residence-keyed county tax (MD [US-MDP51-001]) and work-keyed municipal tax (OH [US-OHP52-003], M) coexist.

Per-jurisdiction tier = the tier of the jurisdiction ANNEX: **T1** no state wage tax (SUI and levies only); **T3** state withholding with no separately remitted local layer found; **T4** a local layer remitted to a separate local authority. ## Federal and cross-cutting questions (the seven mandate questions + oracle, scope, owner)

Every answer needs a citation; at creation (2026-10-29) none was on disk, so all started open for research; all 65 are answered as of 2026-11-02. Owner questions (US-MQ-11, 13, 14) are answered by the owner and recorded the same way.

### Q1 — Tax year
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-01`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Which instruments fix the period of federal employment-tax reporting (Form W-2 / 941 / 940) and of state wage-withholding reconciliation, and may an employer with a non-calendar fiscal year report employment taxes on that fiscal year instead of the calendar year?  
**What would answer it:** Federal: the IRC withholding/reporting provisions and the Treasury regulation on the W-2 period, plus the Form 941/940 instructions (IRS). One state DoR annual-reconciliation instruction as a check that states follow the calendar year.  
**Blocks:** CCG, SCOPE, RSPEC, DD, WTC, SIR, PSL, SKILL; Sets YEAR=2026 (next_year 2027) for every artifact and every jurisdiction annex.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-29 (confidence 88%)  
**Answer:** Federal employment-tax periods are fixed to the calendar year/quarter, and no fiscal-year option exists: FUTA is imposed per calendar year (26 U.S.C. 3301); FICA returns per calendar quarter, Form 944 annual (26 CFR 31.6011(a)-1; Form 941 instr. Rev. 03-2026); W-2 covers remuneration paid during the calendar year, 2026 forms due 2027-02-01 (26 CFR 31.6051-1; W-2/W-3 instr.); Form 940 is a calendar-year return. A fiscal-year employer may NOT report employment taxes on its fiscal year. State check (cross-read from jurisdiction rows, inference not a quoted rule): annual reconciliations fall due in January after the calendar year (DE W-1 recon Jan 31; VT WH-434 last day of January; MS W-2 to DOR 31 Jan). YEAR=2026, next_year=2027.  
**Residual (non-blocking):** No state instruction states 'calendar year' in terms (state limb is an inference from January due dates).  
**register_id:** US-FED-013 (primary); US-FED-015, US-FED-016, US-FED-005, US-FED-006, US-FED-007, US-FED-004; state corroboration US-DEWH-001, US-VTPIT-001, US-MSPIT-002 (resolve in `_register-shards/mandate-FED-MULTI.csv` / jurisdiction shards; merge into `legal-basis-register.csv` pending, agent 2)
**Register check (seal attempt 4, 2026-10-30):** cites US-FED-005, now `superseded` by US-FEDR3-006 (a Pub. 15 farmwork row: wrong supersedes link, agent 2 to correct); the answer rests on the active primary US-FED-013 and on US-FED-015/016, so it stands.

### Q2 — Currency, minor units and rounding
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-02`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Which instrument requires federal employment-tax returns and W-2s to be stated in USD, and what rounding rules apply (a) inside the federal withholding computation (Pub. 15-T methods: rounding of wages or withholding to whole dollars?) and (b) on returns (optional whole-dollar rounding on Forms 941/940/W-2)? Is there any rule fixing a payment unit coarser than USD 0.01?  
**What would answer it:** IRS Pub. 15-T (computation rounding), Form 941/940 instructions (whole-dollar option), General Instructions for Forms W-2 and W-3. State-specific rounding is asked in each jurisdiction question (US-MQ-20..70).  
**Blocks:** CCG, WTC, PSL, RSPEC, DD; rounding.payment / rounding.accounting / rounding.rule; WTC and payslip number formats.  
**Partial findings 2026-10-29 (NOT an answer; confidence 85% on (a)/(b); 0% on the USD instrument):** (a) Computation: Pub. 15-T 2026 allows wages to be rounded to the nearest dollar and the period tax to be rounded to the nearest dollar, OPTIONAL, so exact-cent calculation is permitted [US-FED-001, V]. (b) Returns: Form 941 must show cents, no whole-dollar rounding [US-FED-005, V]; Form 940 whole-dollar rounding optional (2025 edition) [US-FED-007, V]; W-2 expects cents (inferred from the common-errors list, 80%) [US-FED-006, V]. No payment unit coarser than USD 0.01 located. State computation rounding (whole dollar) verified for AL, IA, MS, NC, ND, OK (OR optional): see US-MQ-21/32/45/47/48/56/57.  
**Evidence:** verified: US-FED-001, US-FED-005, US-FED-006, US-FED-007; MANUAL: none; gated: none  
**Still open (blocks the answer):** Instrument requiring USD on federal returns/W-2 (31 U.S.C. 5103 not fetched); W-2 cents rule in terms (W-2/W-3 instr. electronic-filing amounts section unread).  
**Seal attempt 2 (2026-10-29):** Pub. 15-T 2026 rounding text re-read: optional, under 50 cents down, 50-99 up, must be used consistently [US-FED-001, US-FED-027]; Form 941 cents rule stands [US-FED-005]. Still missing: the instrument requiring USD on returns/W-2 and a W-2 cents rule in terms (both NOT LOCATED, inferred only); no rule on a payment unit coarser than 0.01 located (absence is not a citation). STAYS OPEN.  
**Seal attempt 3 (2026-10-30):** answered from agent 2 pass 4/5 (P5-FED-r1/r2) and owner decisions of 2026-10-29.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-30 (seal attempt 3; confidence 90%)  
**Answer:** Currency USD for payroll and reporting: payments of U.S. tax must be remitted to the IRS in U.S. dollars, digital assets not accepted (Pub. 15 2026, Making a tax payment) [US-FEDP52-001]; U.S. coins and currency are legal tender for taxes (31 U.S.C. 5103) [US-FEDP51-001]. Minor units 2: W-2 money amounts must carry the decimal point and cents [US-FEDP51-012]; Form 941 must show cents, no whole-dollar rounding [US-FED-005]; Form 940 whole-dollar rounding optional [US-FED-007]. Computation: Pub. 15-T 2026 lets the employer round wages and the period tax to the nearest dollar (under 50 cents down, 50-99 up), optional and used consistently [US-FED-001]. No payment unit coarser than USD 0.01 exists in the federal instruments read: rounding.payment = rounding.accounting = 0.01. State computation rounding (whole dollar in e.g. AL, MS, NC, ND, OK, MT up; optional in OR, KS, MA; Iowa corrected to the cent, see below) is answered per jurisdiction, part 1. Corrected and extended 2026-11-02 (Stage-5 re-check): Iowa computes 2026 withholding to the CENT, not the dollar (tables 'rounded to the nearest cent'; formula without dollar rounding) [US-IAS5-005, US-IAS5-015]; per-period tax rounded to the whole dollar in AL [US-ALS5-001], MS [US-MSS5-002], ND (an amount under 1.00 need not be withheld) [US-NDS5-001], MO (nearest whole dollar, 49 cents down / 50 up) [US-MOS5-001] and ME [US-MES5-011]; optional dollar rounding in OR [US-ORS5-009], KS [US-KSS5-001], MA [US-MAS5-023], MN [US-MNS5-010]; KY rounds the WAGES (not the tax) to the nearest dollar at the employer's election, KRS 141.310(4) [US-KYS5-010]; LA formula examples and tables in cents, no rounding rule [US-LAS5-001]; VA tables to the nearest dollar, formula exact [US-VAS5-013]. None of these changes rounding.payment / rounding.accounting (0.01): they are computation rules inside the state layer.  
**Residual (non-blocking):** No instrument read states in terms that the 941/W-2 return itself is denominated in USD (follows from the payment rule and the form fields); 26 U.S.C. 6316 (foreign-currency payment by regulation) is discretionary and was not registered.  
**register_id:** US-FEDP52-001 (primary); US-FEDP51-001, US-FEDP51-012, US-FED-001, US-FED-005, US-FED-007; state rounding (added 2026-11-02): US-IAS5-005, US-IAS5-015, US-ALS5-001, US-MSS5-002, US-NDS5-001, US-MOS5-001, US-MES5-011, US-ORS5-009, US-KSS5-001, US-MAS5-023, US-MNS5-010, US-KYS5-010, US-LAS5-001, US-VAS5-013 (cited rows `active` in `legal-basis-register.csv`, 2,533 rows, checked 2026-11-02, Stage-5 re-check)
**Register check (Stage-5 re-check, 2026-11-02):** the federal ids are unchanged and active (US-FED-005 is superseded, as recorded at attempt 4, and the cents rule rests on US-FEDP51-012). The per-state rounding sentence is corrected for Iowa (to the cent; US-IA-001's dollar clause carries an S5A supersession note) and extended with the Stage-5 rows; the local files of US-IAS5-015 (Step 5), US-MOS5-001, US-NDS5-001, US-KYS5-010 and US-LAS5-001 were re-read by the scaffolder for the quoted rounding text. rounding.payment / rounding.accounting unchanged (0.01).  
**Register check (seal attempt 4, 2026-10-30):** cites US-FED-005, now `superseded` by US-FEDR3-006 (wrong supersedes link, agent 2 to correct); the cents rule also rests on the active US-FEDP51-012, so it stands.

### Q3 — Official languages and what fixes document language (G-LANG)
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-03`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Is there a federal or state instrument designating an official language that binds employer documents, and which instrument (if any) fixes the language of (a) the employee pay statement / wage statement and (b) authority-facing filings (IRS, SSA, state DoR, state UI agency)? Which states require wage or pay-rate notices in the employee's primary language, and are bilingual documents permitted? If no statute fixes payslip language, state the non-statutory constraint that governs instead.  
**What would answer it:** Federal: any official-language instrument and the FLSA recordkeeping rule (29 CFR Part 516) for whether a pay statement is federally required at all; per state: the pay-statement content provision and any primary-language notice provision (collected under US-MQ-20..70, summarised here).  
**Blocks:** CCG, PSL, DD; languages.payslip_rule / authority_facing_rule / seams (es candidate); PSL language tabs.  
**Partial findings 2026-10-29 (NOT an answer; confidence 85% federal; state side mostly open):** Federal: English designated official language by presidential action of 2025-03-01, sec. 3, with no obligation on private employers [US-FED-020, V]; 29 CFR 516.2(a) prescribes employer records only, NOT an employee pay statement, and carries no language rule [US-FED-010, V]. So no federal instrument fixes payslip language or requires a federal pay statement; state pay-statement statutes govern content. State language rules located: Minn. Stat. 181.032 hire notice in English with a right to request another language [US-MNPAY-001, V]; NY Labor Law 195(1) primary-language hire notice [US-NY-004, M]. Authority-facing filings: forms and instructions in English; no language statute located.  
**Evidence:** verified: US-FED-020, US-FED-010, US-MNPAY-001; MANUAL: US-NY-004; gated: none  
**Still open (blocks the answer):** Complete list of states with primary-language notice rules; bilingual-document permission; employment-share and sanction axes for G-LANG (first_build en, es seam rank).

**Answered:** 2026-11-01 (seal attempt 6; confidence 90% on 'no source read requires a non-English pay statement'; scope statement by delegated decision)  
**Answer:** Official languages: federal count 1, English, by presidential action of 2025-03-01, which places no obligation on private employers [US-FED-020]; the 13 state designations read (AZ, FL, HI, IA, KS, MO, MT, NC, ND, NE, NH, SC, SD) govern government documents, proceedings or records only (AZ and IA expressly exclude commerce; HI adds Hawaiian for public acts as provided by law; SD names Lakota/Dakota/Nakota as official indigenous language; SC 1-1-697 binds the State only) and none restricts or mandates the language of a private employer's payroll documents [US-AZP9L-002, US-FLP9L-002, US-HIP9L-002, US-IAP9L-002, US-KSP9L-002, US-MOP9L-002, US-MTP9L-002, US-NCP9L-002, US-NDP9L-002, US-NEP9L-002, US-NHP9L-002, US-SCP9L-002, US-SDP9L-002]. (a) payslip_rule: no federal instrument requires a pay statement or fixes its language (29 CFR 516.2(a) prescribes records only [US-FED-010]); state pay-statement statutes fix content, not language; no source read (statute census of 51 jurisdictions, 48/51 postings pages) requires the pay statement itself in a language other than English: payslip in English [US-OWN-006]. (b) authority_facing_rule (kept separate): IRS, SSA and state forms and instructions are in English and no statute fixing filing language was located: authority-facing filings in English [US-OWN-006]. Second-language duties attach to notices and posters only and are modelled as seams: CA 2810.5(a)(1) hire notice in the language the employer normally uses [US-CAP6-001, US-FEDR3-009]; NY 195(1)(a) English + primary language [US-NYP51-003, US-NYP9L-001]; DC 32-1008(c) [US-DCS5-010]; MN 181.032 English, other language on request [US-MNP6-001]; NJ earned-sick-leave notice [US-FEDR3-010]; NJ gender-equity notice, N.J.S.A. 34:11-56.12(c), employers with 50+ employees post and provide it in English, Spanish and any other Commissioner-available first language of a significant number of the workforce, deliverable as a pay-check insert [US-NJP9M-001]; CO COMPS Order Rule 7.4.3, Spanish COMPS Order and poster where employees with limited English speak Spanish, or a Division version in another language; agricultural Rule 2.3.2(C)(3) 5% rule [US-COP9L-001]; CO 8-13.3-408(2) English + first language of 5% of the workforce [US-COL-001]; TX Reporting Workplace Violence notice in English and Spanish, every employer with 1+ employees since 2024-01-08 (HB 915; 40 TAC 800.600) [US-TXP9M-001]; AZ minimum-wage and earned-paid-sick-time posters in English and Spanish, both marked required [US-AZP9M-001], A.R.S. 23-375(B) [US-AZL-001]; CT 31-57w(b) [US-CTL-001]; DE 19 Del. C. 3710(c) [US-DEL-001]; FL 448.109(3)(a) [US-FLL-001]; MA c.149 s.148C(o) [US-MAL-002]; MI MCL 408.968 [US-MIL-001]; OR 652.610(6) [US-ORL-001]; TX 61.006 [US-TXL-001]; VT 342(f) payroll-card disclosures [US-VTL-001]; WI 103.915(8) migrant workers [US-WIL-001]. Express negatives: IN [US-INP9L-001]; NC, English the legal minimum [US-NCP9L-001]; DE wage-hour poster [US-DEP9L-001]; WA, Spanish portion not required [US-WAP9M-001]. Bilingual documents permitted: no read source in any jurisdiction prohibits a bilingual payroll document; express permission IN, DE, NC, KS (agencies), ME [US-INP9L-001, US-DEP9L-001, US-NCP9L-001, US-KSP9L-002, US-MEP9L-001]. G-LANG (L1): first_build en, on law (English is the only federal official language and the base language of every state rule located, so the workplace employment-share axis cannot change the result; sanction axis empty: no nullity of a payslip for its language found); es rank 2 = mandatory-later for the statutory and agency notice/poster templates above, documented seam for the payslip itself; other languages (NJ Commissioner and CO Division languages, NY/DC primary-language templates, CO and DE 5% rules) = documented seams keyed to state templates, ranked after es because each is a per-state template, not a payroll calculation.
**New seams (US-OWN-006, verbatim):** NJ gender-equity notice (34:11-56.12(c), 50+ employees, en/es + Commissioner languages), CO COMPS Rule 7.4.3 (Spanish COMPS/poster where LEP Spanish speakers; rule quoted on agency page), TX workplace-violence notice en/es (HB 915, 40 TAC 800.600), AZ en+es minimum-wage/sick-time posters.
**Recorded limits (US-OWN-006, verbatim):** CA, DC, FL postings pages unreadable in browser (CA 2810.5 and DC 32-1008(c) already read via R3); GA Act 185 (2019) text not opened; MS 71-1-35 not read on an official host (MS has no pay-statement statute); CO rule instrument not read; official-language designations read in 13 states, all government-only.

### Q4 — Sub-national wrinkle: confirm the T4 discriminator
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-04`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Confirm with citations that (a) federal income-tax withholding, FICA and FUTA are computed and remitted to the IRS on federal returns (941/944, 940), and (b) state income-tax withholding and state unemployment insurance are separate calculations remitted to separate state authorities on separate returns, i.e. no state tax is absorbed into the federal deduction. Does the FUTA credit for state UI contributions (and credit-reduction states) create a calculation dependency between the federal and state layers?  
**What would answer it:** IRC chapter 24 (wage withholding) and chapters 21/23 (FICA/FUTA) including the FUTA credit provision; one representative state withholding statute and one state UI statute.  
**Blocks:** CCG, SCOPE, RSPEC, DD, WTC, SIR, PSL, SKILL; tier.overall=T4 (provisional until answered); fan-out design of the whole build.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-29 (confidence 90%)  
**Answer:** Discriminator confirmed. (a) 26 U.S.C. 3402(a)(1): every employer paying wages withholds federal income tax under IRS tables/procedures; the section covers federal tax only [US-FED-012]; FICA/FUTA are reported on federal returns 941/944/940 [US-FED-005, US-FED-007, US-FED-015]; FUTA 6% per calendar year [US-FED-013]. (b) State withholding is a separate state-law calculation (Va. Code 58.1-461: withhold an amount approximating annual state liability after state exemptions/standard deduction [US-VAPIT-002]) remitted to the state revenue authority on its own returns (Virginia Tax VA-5/VA-6, filing even at zero [US-VAPIT-003]); state UI is a separate state tax base and agency with its own report (Fla. Stat. 443.1216 [US-MULTI-001]; SC Code ch. 41-31 employer contributions, not deductible from wages (41-31-310) [US-SCS5-015]; MD Labor report and pay within one month after quarter end [US-MDSUI-001]). No state tax is absorbed into the federal deduction. (c) FUTA credit dependency: YES. 26 U.S.C. 3302(a)(1) credits state UI contributions, (c)(1) caps credits at 90%, (c)(2) reduces the credit in Title XII credit-reduction states [US-FED-014]. Tier: overall T4; futa T3.  
**Residual (non-blocking):** 2026 credit-reduction state list (DOL, published November; Form 940 Schedule A) is a stage-1 data item, not a mandate blocker.  
**register_id:** US-FED-012 (primary); US-FED-013, US-FED-014, US-FED-005, US-FED-007, US-FED-015, US-VAPIT-002, US-VAPIT-003, US-MULTI-001, US-SCS5-015, US-MDSUI-001 (cited rows `active` in `legal-basis-register.csv`, 2,533 rows, checked 2026-11-02, Stage-5 re-check)
**Register check (Stage-5 re-check, 2026-11-02):** US-SC-002 superseded at the S5 merge by US-SCS5-015 (same SC Code ch. 41-31 local file; 41-31-310 'contributions shall not be deducted from wages' re-read by the scaffolder in `ccg-sources/SC/SC-Code_41-31_UI-contributions.html`); re-pointed here and in the Answer. Discriminator unchanged.

### Q6 — Indexation (federal)
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-06`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Name each federal indexation mechanism that moves 2026/2027 payroll parameters, with the index, its caliber, the trigger and the step: (a) income-tax brackets / standard deduction (which CPI variant, which annual Revenue Procedure); (b) the Social Security contribution and benefit base (which wage index, which SSA announcement); (c) other indexed federal payroll limits (retirement deferrals, HSA, fringe exclusions).  
**What would answer it:** The IRC inflation-adjustment provision plus the IRS Revenue Procedure for 2026 (and 2027 when published); the Social Security Act provision on the contribution base plus the SSA announcement.  
**Blocks:** CCG, WTC, DD; Agent 2 G-DERIVE proofs; 2027 next_year refresh.  
**Partial findings 2026-10-29 (NOT an answer; confidence 98% on SS base; 95% on Rev. Proc. amounts; 70% on CPI caliber):** (a) Rev. Proc. 2025-32 (post-OBBBA, Pub. L. 119-21) sets 2026 items by reference to IRC 1(f): standard deduction MFJ 32,200 / HoH 24,150 / single and MFS 16,100; transit/parking 340/month; health FSA 3,400, carryover 680 [US-FED-002, V]. (b) OASDI contribution and benefit base 184,500 for 2026 (90 FR 49047); SSA Act 230(b) formula reproduced: 60,600 x 69,846.57 / 22,935.42 = 184,548.71 -> 184,500 (multiple of 300) [US-FED-003, V; Pub. 15 2026 corroborates, US-FED-004]. State wage bases indexed: NY 18% of state average annual wage rule [US-NY-007, M]; WY 2026/2027 bases published [US-WYSUI-001, V].  
**Evidence:** verified: US-FED-002, US-FED-003, US-FED-004, US-WYSUI-001; MANUAL: US-NY-007; gated: none  
**Still open (blocks the answer):** CPI caliber (IRC 1(f)(3) chained CPI-U, statute not fetched); 402(g)/HSA/other indexed limits; 2027 SSA base and 2027 Rev. Proc.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-29 (seal attempt 2; confidence 90%)  
**Answer:** (a) Income-tax brackets, standard deduction and other 26 U.S.C. 1(f)-linked items: index = chained CPI-U (C-CPI-U) for the preceding calendar year over the 2016 base, 26 U.S.C. 1(f)(3) [US-FED-026]; trigger = annual, published in an IRS Revenue Procedure (2026: Rev. Proc. 2025-32, post-OBBBA [US-FED-002]); 2026 values: standard deduction MFJ 32,200 / HoH 24,150 / single and MFS 16,100; 132(f) transit and parking 340/month; health FSA 3,400, carryover 680 [US-FED-002, US-FED-025]. (b) OASDI contribution and benefit base: Social Security Act 230(b), prior base x ratio of the national average wage index, rounded to a multiple of 300; 2026 = 184,500 (60,600 x 69,846.57 / 22,935.42 = 184,548.71 -> 184,500) [US-FED-003]; 2027 = 192,300 already published by SSA [US-FED-022]; Medicare (HI) has no base [US-FED-022]. (c) Other indexed federal payroll limits announced annually: 402(g) elective deferral 2026 = 24,500, catch-up 8,000, ages 60-63 11,250 [US-FED-023]; HSA 4,400 self-only / 8,750 family, Rev. Proc. 2025-19 [US-FED-024]; 911 exclusion 132,900 [US-FED-025]. State wage-base indexation is answered per jurisdiction (US-MQ-20..70, part 3).  
**Residual (non-blocking):** US-FED-026 is a republication of the statute (A.3, LII), not uscode.house.gov; the 1(f)(7) per-item rounding step was not read, so stage-1 G-DERIVE uses the published Rev. Proc. values rather than re-computing them; 2027 Rev. Proc. and 2027 limits other than the OASDI base are next_year data items; the 0.9% Additional Medicare threshold was not read.  
**register_id:** US-FED-026 (primary, caliber); US-FED-002, US-FED-003, US-FED-022, US-FED-023, US-FED-024, US-FED-025, US-FED-004 (resolve in `_register-shards/mandate-FED-MULTI.csv`; merge into `legal-basis-register.csv` pending, agent 2)

### Q7 — Collective agreements
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-07`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Does any US instrument extend a collective agreement's pay terms to non-signatory employers in a sector, or do CBAs bind only the certified bargaining unit? Are federal/state prevailing-wage regimes (public-works contracts) in scope for a private-sector payroll build? Which sectors, if any, are in scope?  
**What would answer it:** NLRA (29 U.S.C. 151 ff.) on exclusive representation; Davis-Bacon Act; per-state prevailing-wage statutes under US-MQ-20..70.  
**Blocks:** CCG, SCOPE; collective_agreements tier and CCG m11 scope.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-29 (confidence 90%)  
**Answer:** No US instrument extends a collective agreement's pay terms to non-signatory employers in a sector: under 29 U.S.C. 159(a) the majority representative is the exclusive representative of the employees in the appropriate unit only [US-FED-018]. Prevailing wage is a contract-level overlay, not a sector rule: Davis-Bacon, 40 U.S.C. 3142, applies to federal construction contracts over USD 2,000 with Labor Secretary wage determinations, paid at least weekly [US-FED-019]. For this private-sector build: CBAs bind only the certified unit (client data, not statutory tables); prevailing wage OUT OF SCOPE except as an optional flag for covered public-works contracts (owner to confirm the flag). No sector is in scope by operation of law. collective_agreements = T1 confirmed. State prevailing-wage statutes are carried per jurisdiction (US-MQ-20..70, part 6) and do not change this answer.  
**register_id:** US-FED-018 (primary); US-FED-019 (resolve in `_register-shards/mandate-FED-MULTI.csv` / jurisdiction shards; merge into `legal-basis-register.csv` pending, agent 2)

### Scope fact — Third-party filing provider (SIR)
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-09`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** Scope fact (owner, biweekly 2026-10-21): statutory filings go through a THIRD-PARTY filing provider; no ADP integration. Research: which instruments authorise a third party to file and deposit federal employment taxes for the employer (reporting-agent authorisation, Form 8655 and its Revenue Procedure), what liability stays with the employer, and what is each state's equivalent authorisation (POA / agent form) for withholding and UI filings? Owner: which provider, and what input format does it accept?  
**What would answer it:** IRS reporting-agent Revenue Procedure and Form 8655 instructions; per-state POA/agent forms under US-MQ-20..70; owner answer for the provider.  
**Blocks:** SIR, RSPEC, SCOPE; SIR scope: Mercans produces provider input files, not authority submissions.  
**Partial findings 2026-10-29 (NOT an answer; confidence 90% federal):** Rev. Proc. 2012-32 governs Form 8655 Reporting Agent Authorization: the agent may sign and e-file Forms 940/941 and make federal deposits; the authorization does NOT relieve the employer of liability for timely filing and deposits (sec. 5.05); the agent must say so in writing and recommend EFTPS enrolment [US-FED-008, V]. Form 8655 Rev. 01-2024; Pub. 1474 (Apr 2026) and Rev. Proc. 2012-33 related [US-FED-009, V]. Section 3504 agents / CPEOs (Schedule R) and Form 2678 agents are distinct appointments (not analysed). MN bulk filers need each client's Minnesota ID [US-MNPIT-001, V]. SIR scope fact stands: Mercans produces provider input; employer keeps liability.  
**Evidence:** verified: US-FED-008, US-FED-009, US-MNPIT-001; MANUAL: none; gated: none  
**Still open (blocks the answer):** State POA/agent forms (all 51); OWNER: provider name and accepted input format.  
**Seal attempt 2 (2026-10-29):** Form 8655 Rev. 1-2024 read: lines 15/16 list returns and deposits, line 19 extends to state/local returns tied to them; employer stays liable [US-FED-028]; Rev. Proc. 2012-32 sec. 5.05 quarterly written notice [US-FED-008]; Pub. 15 2026 third-party payer section [US-FED-029]. Federal limb answerable. Still missing: state POA/agent forms (none of the 51 answered; only WV UI POA form existence noted, UT POA notarization from 2026-06-01 title-only) and OWNER: provider name and accepted input format. STAYS OPEN.  
**Seal attempt 3 (2026-10-30):** answered from agent 2 pass 4/5 (P5-FED-r1/r2) and owner decisions of 2026-10-29.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-30 (seal attempt 3; confidence 90% (federal); owner limb by owner decision)  
**Answer:** Federal: Form 8655 / Rev. Proc. 2012-32 let a reporting agent sign and e-file Forms 940/941 and make federal deposits; line 19 extends the authorisation to state/local returns tied to them; the authorisation does NOT relieve the employer of liability for timely filing and deposit, and the agent must give the quarterly written notice (sec. 5.05) [US-FED-008, US-FED-028, US-FED-009]; third-party payer arrangements per Pub. 15 2026 [US-FEDP51-002] (re-pointed at seal attempt 6). Owner limb: OWNER DECISION 2026-10-29 (relayed by the orchestrator): the choice of filing provider is a business decision outside this build; the SIR documents the provider-agnostic hand-off process (`Research Pipeline/USA/00-filing-terceiros/PROCESSO-FILING-TERCEIROS.md`); Mercans produces provider input, the employer keeps liability. **Residual (non-blocking):** EFW2 TY2026 (version, record layouts, signature/PIN) is a SIR stage-6 data item, not a mandate item: https://www.ssa.gov/employer/efw/26efw2.pdf is unreadable/gated (US-FEDP52-013, US-FIL-003); the state POA census stays open where part 6 is open in a jurisdiction question.

### Q4 — Multi-state domain
**Status:** `answered`  
**Question id:** `US-MQ-10`  
**Asked:** 2026-10-29  
**Question:** For employees who live in one state and work in another (or in several): what decides (a) which state's income tax is withheld (work-state sourcing vs residence), (b) reciprocity agreements (which state pairs), (c) resident credit for tax paid to the work state and whether the employer must withhold the difference, (d) convenience-of-the-employer rules (which states), (e) day/dollar thresholds before nonresident withholding starts (which states, what threshold), (f) the SUI localization tests (localized / base of operations / direction and control / residence) and the reciprocal coverage election? Is there any federal statute limiting state taxation of mobile employees?  
**What would answer it:** The uniform UI localization provision as enacted in a state UI law; each state's nonresident-withholding provision and reciprocity agreement text (collected under US-MQ-20..70); any limiting federal statute in Title 4 U.S.C.  
**Blocks:** CCG, DD, WTC, SCOPE; multistate design and DD master-data keys (work_location_jurisdiction, residence_jurisdiction, sui_state).  
**Partial findings 2026-10-29 (NOT an answer; confidence 60%):** (b) Reciprocity, verified rows: KY-IL/IN/MI/WV/WI, VA (daily commute), OH (non-shareholder) [US-KY-001]; PA-IN/MD/NJ/OH/VA/WV via REV-419 [US-PA-001]; NJ-PA [US-NJ-001]; VA-DC/KY/MD/PA/WV [US-VAPIT-004]; WI-IL/IN/KY/MI, MN special [US-WIPIT-001]; MN-MI/ND via MWR [US-MNPIT-001]; ND-MN/MT via NDW-R [US-ND-002]; DE none [US-DEWH-001]. MANUAL: IN, OH, WV, AZ, CT lists [US-IN-002, US-OH-001, US-WVPIT-002, US-AZWH-002, US-CTMS-001]; US-MULTI-002 is a lead list, not a source. (d) Convenience rule: NY Tax Law 631 [US-NY-006, M; US-MULTI-003, M], CT reciprocal convenience rule [US-CTMS-001, M]. (e) Thresholds: AL 30-day nonresident safe harbor, Act 2025-334 [US-ALWH-001, V]; CO physical-presence sourcing [US-COWH-002, V]; ND withhold when services performed in ND [US-ND-002, V]. (f) SUI localization: Fla. Stat. 443.1216(7)/(9)/(12) localized-service rule and reciprocal arrangement [US-MULTI-001, V]; base-of-operations / direction-and-control / residence tail not read.  
**Evidence:** verified: US-KY-001, US-PA-001, US-NJ-001, US-VAPIT-004, US-WIPIT-001, US-MNPIT-001, US-ND-002, US-DEWH-001, US-ALWH-001, US-COWH-002, US-MULTI-001; MANUAL: US-MULTI-002, US-MULTI-003, US-NY-006, US-IN-002, US-OH-001, US-WVPIT-002, US-AZWH-002, US-CTMS-001; gated: none  
**Still open (blocks the answer):** (a) uniform work-state vs residence sourcing statement; (c) resident credit and difference withholding; (d) convenience rule in other states; (e) thresholds in other states; (f) localization tail; federal mobile-employee statutes (49 USC 14503/40116, 46 USC 11108, 4 USC 114 unproven leads).  
**Seal attempt 2 (2026-10-29):** Reciprocity now answered from each state's own source for 17 jurisdictions (AZ, DC, IA, KY, MD, MI, MN, ND, NJ, OH, PA, VA, WI, WV, DE none; IL, IN, MT only from partner states) [US-MULTI-004..019, US-MULTI-002]; convenience rule NY [US-MULTI-003], NJ [US-MULTI-012], CT [US-MULTI-020]; thresholds AZ 60 days [US-MULTI-017], AL 30 days [US-MULTI-021], WV 30 days [US-MULTI-015], DC 183 days [US-MULTI-016], CO day-apportioned [US-MULTI-023]. Still missing: (a) uniform work-state vs residence sourcing statement; (c) resident credit / difference withholding (no uniform rule; only WI, MD, MO W-4C seen); (d) convenience rule in other states (PA, AR, NE, DE NOT LOCATED); (e) thresholds elsewhere; (f) SUI localization tail beyond Fla. Stat. 443.1216; federal mobile-worker statutes not attempted. STAYS OPEN.  
**Seal attempt 3 (2026-10-30):** answered from agent 2 pass 4/5 (P5-FED-r1/r2) and owner decisions of 2026-10-29.  
**Answered:** 2026-10-30 (seal attempt 3; confidence 80%)  
**Answer:** (a) No federal rule fixes work-state vs residence sourcing for wages in general: each state taxes residents on all wages (with a credit for tax paid to the work state) and nonresidents on wages for in-state services; the per-state rule is answered in US-MQ-20..70, part 5. (b)-(e) Reciprocity pairs, resident credit and difference withholding, convenience-of-employer rules (NY, NJ, CT, NE) and nonresident day/dollar thresholds (e.g. AL 30 days, AZ 60 days, DC 183 days, ME 12 days + 3,000, MT 30 days, NE 7 days, NM 15 days, ID 1,000 USD) are carried per jurisdiction, part 5 (closed in 50 of 51; SC open under US-MQ-60). (f) SUI localization: four tests in order (1) localized service, (2) base of operations, (3) place of direction or control, (4) residence; enacted in all state UI laws from the DOL model in the 1940s [US-FEDP52-005]; statutory instance Fla. Stat. 443.1216 [US-MULTI-001]; current DOL restatements UIPL 20-04 and the OUI memo [US-MULTIS5-016, US-MULTIS5-017]. Federal limits on state taxation of mobile employees: motor-carrier employees with regular duties in 2+ states are taxable only by the residence state, 49 U.S.C. 14503 [US-MULTIS5-001]; rail employees likewise, 49 U.S.C. 11502 [US-MULTIS5-002]; air-carrier employees: residence state plus the state where more than 50% of pay is earned, 49 U.S.C. 40116(f) [US-MULTIS5-003]; seamen: no state withholding, 46 U.S.C. 11108 [US-MULTIS5-004]; military spouses keep their residence, 50 U.S.C. 4001 [US-MULTIS5-005]; 4 U.S.C. 114 covers retirement income only, not wages [US-FEDP51-009]. No general federal statute limits state taxation of mobile employees (S. 1443 / H.R. 10271 not enacted). Added 2026-11-02 (Stage-5 re-check): (e) LA nonresident 30-or-fewer-days exemption from 2026-01-01, Act 382 of 2025 [US-MULTIS5-021], rule adopted LR 52:358 effective 2026-03-20 [US-LAS5-002]; IL withholds on nonresidents by UI-style localization and, for non-localized service, from more than 30 working days in Illinois (Pub. 130 R-02/26) [US-MULTIS5-023]; (c) MS DOES have a resident-working-elsewhere withholding rule: residents are withheld on total wages, but wages for services in another state may be excluded to the extent income tax is withheld for that state, otherwise MS withholds on all wages [US-MSS5-004, US-MSS5-019] (the MULTI synthesis table 2.2 that lists MS under credit-at-assessment-only is wrong on this point, agent 2 to correct). The reciprocity matrix is carried on the state-source rows US-MULTI-004..020 (MULTI.md 2.3); the US-MULTIS5 rows hold the federal limits, localization, convenience and threshold rules.  
**Residual (non-blocking):** The DOL Comparison edition read reflects law as of 2023-01-01; state citations for the localization tests are per state (only Florida read in terms).  
**register_id:** US-FEDP52-005 (primary); US-MULTIS5-001, US-MULTIS5-002, US-MULTIS5-003, US-MULTIS5-004, US-MULTIS5-005, US-MULTIS5-016, US-MULTIS5-017, US-MULTIS5-021, US-LAS5-002, US-MULTIS5-023, US-MSS5-004, US-MSS5-019, US-FEDP51-009, US-MULTI-001 (cited rows `active` in `legal-basis-register.csv`, 2,533 rows, checked 2026-11-02, Stage-5 re-check)
**Register check (Stage-5 re-check, 2026-11-02):** US-FEDP51-007/-008 (LII copies) superseded by the GPO-text rows US-MULTIS5-001/-002; added US-MULTIS5-003 (air), -004 (seamen), -005 (military spouses), -016/-017 (DOL localization restatements), the LA 30-day rule (US-MULTIS5-021 + US-LAS5-002), IL Pub. 130 (US-MULTIS5-023) and the MS resident rule (US-MSS5-004, US-MSS5-019). Answer extended, not reversed.  
**Register check (seal attempt 6, 2026-11-01):** register_id list unchanged and active. The summary '(b)-(e) closed in 50 of 51' is stale: CT (US-MQ-26) and OR (US-MQ-57) part 5 are reopened at attempt 6 because their resident/nonresident rows moved to MANUAL under the P9-MERGE WebFetch ruling; read it as 'closed in 48 of 51; open CT, OR, SC'. The federal limbs of this answer are not affected.

**Seal attempt 6 close (2026-11-02): part 5 ANSWERED** on US-CTP10-001 (Circular CT IP 2026(1), official PDF held at `00-official-sources/pdfs/CT/CT_IP-2026-1_2026-11-02.pdf`, md5 a64b2bbb855163fb832396939efc74ef, re-read 2026-11-02 pp. 7-9).  
**Answer (all six parts; part 5 re-answered 2026-11-02):** 1 TPG-211 eff. 2026-01-01, CT-W4 codes A-F, tables A-E (no percentage method). 2 No municipal wage tax on any DRS list: the only wage-based tax is the state Withholding Tax (residual: the legal basis that municipalities hold only expressly conferred powers rests on the OLR 2017-R-0237 report, now MANUAL). 3 Wage base 27,000, new employer 1.9%, fund solvency tax 1.0%; no employee UI contribution (employee contributions exist only in AK, NJ, PA). 4 CT Paid Leave 0.5% employee to the SS base. 5 (i) Residents: all wages are subject to CT withholding even when working outside CT; where the employee works for the employer in one or more qualifying jurisdictions and the employer has an office or transacts business there, CT tax on total wages is prorated among the jurisdictions and only the excess over the other jurisdiction's required withholding is remitted to DRS (IP 2026(1) p. 7, Example 4). (ii) Nonresidents: wages for services rendered in CT are subject to withholding, allocated on CT-W4NA; 15-day rule: no withholding for 15 days or less in CT in the calendar year, but once more than 15 days are worked, withhold on all CT-service pay including the first 15 days (p. 8, PS 2015(6)). (iii) No reciprocity agreement appears in IP 2026(1) (full-text search 'reciproc': none). Reciprocal convenience-of-employer rule [US-MULTI-020]. 6 Pay statement C.G.S. 31-13a; prevailing wage 31-53; DRS POA LGL-001/LGL-003; UI Certificate of Representation (TPA).

The nonresident threshold (no withholding when Oregon earnings stay under the standard deduction), the out-of-state-employer rule and the reciprocity statement have no active successor: US-ORP51-002 carries the 2026 standard deduction 2,910 but not the nonresident rule. **Question (part 5, stable id US-MQ-57):** When must an employer withhold Oregon tax from a nonresident working in Oregon (standard-deduction threshold, 2026 amount), what applies to an out-of-state employer of Oregon residents, and does Oregon have any reciprocity agreement? **What would answer it:** the official DOR publication 150-206-692 (2026 edition) saved under `00-official-sources/ccg-sources/OR/` and re-read by agent 2 into an `active` A.2 row. Parts 1-4 and 6 stand.
**Seal attempt 6 close (2026-11-02): part 5 ANSWERED** on US-ORP10-001 (DOR 150-206-692, the edition served at the official URL on 2026-11-02 is Rev. 02-08-22; official PDF held at `00-official-sources/ccg-sources/OR/OR_150-206-692_2026-11-02.pdf`, md5 bec23f2ec6484b29c1760ec2243c26fe, re-read 2026-11-02; supersedes US-ORP51-001). No 2026 edition exists; the publication itself says the standard-deduction figures change yearly, so the 2026 amount is taken from US-ORP51-002.  
**Answer (all six parts; part 5 re-answered 2026-11-02):** 1 OR-W-4, formula eff. 2026-01-01, supplemental 8% option, rounding allowed not required, DOR calculator. 2 Metro SHS 1% and Multnomah PFA 1.5%(+1.5%) withheld and filed with the City of Portland on separate returns (T4). 3 Combined Payroll Report, UI base 56,700. 4 Paid Leave 1.0% to 184,500; TriMet/Lane employer taxes; Statewide Transit Tax 0.1% employee. 5 Every employer withholds from resident and nonresident employees working in Oregon. Nonresidents: withhold on all wages for services performed in Oregon unless Oregon earnings for the year will be less than the standard deduction for the filing status (2026: 2,910 single, US-ORS5-009); wages remain Oregon-source on the W-2. Out-of-state employer of Oregon residents working in Oregon (incl. telecommuting from home): withhold on all wages; relief possible if each such employee gets 300 or less in the calendar year. Employer with no employees working in Oregon: withholding for Oregon residents cannot be required (requested as a courtesy). No reciprocity agreement stated (full-text search 'recipro': none); double taxation is handled by the employee's resident credit on the return, not by the employer. Statewide transit tax: residents on all wages, nonresidents on Oregon-service wages. 6 Pay statement ORS 652.610(1) items (A)-(M); BOLI prevailing wage, WH-38; POA OR-AUTH-REP / OR-AUTH-INFO.  
**register_id:** US-OR-001 (primary); per part: 1: US-OR-001; 2: US-ORS5-004; 3: US-OR-002; 4: US-OR-002; 5: US-ORP10-001, US-ORS5-009; 6: US-ORS5-026, US-ORS5-019, US-ORP51-004, US-ORP51-005 (cited rows `active` in `legal-basis-register.csv`, 2,533 rows, checked 2026-11-02, Stage-5 re-check)
